#3030. Worried About a Market Crash? This AI Stock Should Bounce Back Fastest.

AI stocks with strong fundamentals should rebound quickly from a market crash.

By Ryan Vanzo – Nov 21, 2026 at 1:27PM EST

Many of the world's most valuable companies today are artificial intelligence (AI) stocks. That's for good reason. The global AI opportunity is truly massive, and many AI hyperscalers are already quite profitable. But what will happen to these companies during a market crash?

Only around 10% of the S&P 500's value is tied directly to companies engaged in developing AI software. However, between 40% and 50% of the index's value is indirectly exposed to AI. That includes major GPU producers such as Nvidia (NVDA -14.01%), as well as AI-enabled software providers like Palo Alto Networks and ServiceNow.

In short, if markets fall, there's a very strong chance AI stocks will lead the way. It would be no surprise to see AI stocks struggle in a market downturn. While high growth rates may support premium valuations, companies with high growth rates already priced into shares typically falter as markets rerate risk tolerances. But as a recent report from BlackRock points out:

If market sentiment becomes risk-averse, pressuring AI stock valuations, investors would be wise to peruse the bargain bin for deals. There's one AI stock I'm keeping a very close eye on, as its underlying fundamentals and already cheap valuation should make it a strong buy during a market crash.

During market crashes or economic downturns, companies with strong fundamentals often have an opportunity to grow stronger. That's because weaker competitors may face financial difficulties and exit the market. Acquisition prices get cheaper, with expansion opportunities facing less competition.

When conducting a meta-analysis of firms entering a downturn with strong financials and end-market demand, McKinsey & Co. found that these businesses "emerged much stronger coming out of the downturn, and the advantage they gained lasted for a long period."

With this in mind, it's not hard to see Nvidia as a strong choice -- perhaps the strongest choice -- for investors during a market downturn. There is no denying that Nvidia's core business is exposed to long-term growth tailwinds. Both sales and earnings are currently growing at roughly 100% year over year. Growth rates should reach 50% to 100% annually over the coming years.

GPUs are critical components for the AI revolution. Even if Nvidia's leadership position weakens, resulting in lower market shares and compressed margins, top-line growth should remain in the double digits throughout the rest of the decade, perhaps even longer.

Recent headwinds, however -- including an abrupt and controversial CEO transition, potential regulatory pressures, and allegations of circular financing -- have pushed Nvidia's price-to-earnings ratio down to just 16.2, with a forward price-to-earnings ratio of just 9.6.

Nvidia generated $43 billion in free cash flow last quarter. And the company currently has $227 billion remaining on an upscaled share repurchase plan.

Despite near-term concerns, Nvidia remains a financial powerhouse that manufactures critical components essential to the AI industry's expansion. In the event of a downturn, the company can repurchase shares at a discount, reinvest heavily in research and development, and acquire competitors at bargain prices.

It's not clear how much cheaper Nvidia shares can get from here. The company's current fundamentals and near-term growth forecasts already make shares a buy. If a marketwide downturn hits, however, many longer-term concerns, such as eventual margin compression, may become fully priced into shares.

There's still a lot we don't know about Nvidia's future, especially in today's environment. But what we do know is that the company is completely self-funded; benefits from strong, secular growth tailwinds; and has the financial might to outinvest and perhaps even outright acquire much of its competition, barring the internal divisions of hyperscalers.

I'm already strongly considering an investment in Nvidia. Oddly enough, a market downturn could actually strengthen that conviction.

#3027. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

Creator onboarding is management's bet on feeding video supply, but the article leaves open whether that can outpace weakening ad demand.

Advertising budget contractions across key consumer verticals present an operational hurdle for long-term investors evaluating platform monetization. Three things management said stand out.

Advertisers haven't expanded overall marketing budgets, yet targeted content marketing solutions continue to draw brand spending. The focus on celebrity partnerships and product launch campaigns helps stabilize revenue when broader ad categories contract.

Ad spending faces persistent pressure from cautious clients, but executive execution targets high-probability marketing budgets. The platform relies on its trending topic network, real-time social discussion, and interest-based communities to sustain user traffic through volatile cycles.

Capital efficiency remains essential while the business balances disciplined cost controls against targeted core investments.

#3021. FitLife Brands: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Margin protection from forward buying and the Amazon packaging shift both depend on execution, and the article leaves that open.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 21, 2026 at 1:31PM EST

10 stocks we like better than FitLife Brands ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 16, 2026, FitLife Brands (FTLF -7.46%) announced a 9% year-over-year revenue increase to $25.6 million alongside operational cost cuts. Management continues to navigate soft demand in specialty retail channels and pressure on raw material costs across its nutritional supplement portfolio. Three things management said stand out.

Management plans to start shifting some high-volume products to plastic containers for e-commerce order fulfillment, likely starting late this year or early next year, to reduce this expense.

Quarterly performance across the combined business remains stable, providing leadership space to fix fulfillment friction and monitor inventory execution.

Executive strategy prioritizes digital growth on Amazon while maintaining financial discipline and paying down outstanding debt and balance sheet obligations.

#3020. Could Investing $500 a Month in Nebius Help You Retire a Millionaire?

The neocloud stock looks like a steal at current levels.

By Marc Guberti – Nov 21, 2026 at 1:37PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Nebius Group ›

Holding on to Nebius (NBIS -16.11%) stock recently has required an incredible conviction, but that resolve could pay off for long-term investors. Nebius' fundamentals remain robust and continue to strengthen despite its shares losing more than 40% of their value over the past month.

Those types of mismatches create long-term opportunities for investors who can see beyond the short-term noise and focus on the big picture.

Although Nebius delivered a solid earnings report, it's good to deal with the blemish first, especially with the stock’s declines over the past month taking a firm grip on current sentiment.

The neocloud had to delay the delivery of some new capacity due to issues with a third-party provider. That wasn’t Nebius' fault, and the company is building its future data centers to avoid third-party risk moving forward. The end result is that Nebius says it will not hit the $7 billion annual recurring revenue (ARR) target it forecast for December until February.

Anyone who is holding Nebius stock is doing so for the long run. While this two-month delay ruined short-term call options, it didn't affect the long-term thesis much. Artificial intelligence is still heating up, with demand scaling upward.

Financing and profitability have been among the main concerns people have about the neocloud, but Nebius squashed them both in its third-quarter shareholder letter. Its new contracts provide prepayments that cover all capital expenditures. Furthermore, the company expects to be breaking even on a pre-tax basis by 2027.

Within five to 10 years, it's possible that the conversation will shift from Nebius raising money to management initiating stock buybacks. It's quite feasible, since the company's payback period for new data centers has dropped to one year and seven months, and that excludes prepayments.

Its annual contract value per megawatt now exceeds $25 million for long-term deals, and Nebius wasted no time mentioning key enterprise AI leaders that recently became new customers.

Nebius believes it can generate $12 billion to $13 billion in ARR by the end of 2027. Granted, unforeseen delays could push some of that revenue out into 2028, as investors witnessed when Nebius said it wouldn’t reach its $7 billion ARR target until February.

Importantly, though, that guidance shows that growth is not slowing down. In Q3, Nebius delivered 571% year-over-year revenue growth to $981 million. That was also a 68% sequential growth rate from the second quarter.

Nebius has said that it expects to be operating at breakeven on a pre-tax basis by the end of 2027, and that its data centers are recouping the capital investments required to bring them online in just 19 months. However, that's still underselling Nebius as an investment.

One key detail investors may be overlooking is how negotiations will change when it's time to renew contracts. Nebius has to negotiate prepayments to fund its data centers. Receiving capital up front makes it more viable to build data centers at scale.

Customers make prepayments in exchange for lower annual contract values for each megawatt. Once Nebius operates data centers that have been fully paid off, it won't be as reliant on prepayments. That will give Nebius the flexibility to set higher prices per megawatt, and the soaring demand for compute implies that the elevated prices will stick.

Nebius revealed in its Q2 shareholder letter that it gets annual contract values of $40 million to $50 million per megawatt for short-term deals. Customers aren't afraid of those prices, and long-term deals may get closer to those levels once prepayments are no longer necessary.

This detail, combined with Nebius' current performance and pipeline, suggests it can produce 100% or higher year-over-year revenue growth for multiple years. That's a pretty ambitious prediction, but Nebius has been delivering on that minimum growth rate in recent quarters. Guidance implies that ARR will roughly quadruple in a single year.

#3012. The S&P 500 Heads Into Thanksgiving Below Its Peak. Here's How the Holiday Week Has Usually Gone Since 1950.

It has usually been a good week for stocks. Has it been when the index started below a record?

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 21, 2026 at 1:47PM EST

10 stocks we like better than S&P 500 Index ›

The S&P 500 (^GSPC -3.70%) finished on Nov. 16 at 7,416.59, around 5% under the all-time closing high of 7,818.93 it set on Oct. 6. The drop sped up in late October, and the index fell over 4% on Nov. 5 alone as tech stocks sold off.

Chipmakers were among the worst hit that day, amid worries that the midterm election results might slow the artificial intelligence (AI) build-out.

The market is closed on Thursday, Nov. 26, for Thanksgiving, and it closes early (at 1 p.m. ET) the next day.

Historically, this has been a better-than-average week to hold stocks. Since 1950, the index has climbed during Thanksgiving week in 53 of 76 years, or about 70% of the time, and the week's average gain has been roughly 0.7%. By comparison, normal weeks across the same stretch rose 56% of the time and gained roughly 0.2% on average.

But the record looks weaker in years like this one. In the 13 years the index entered the week 3% to 10% under a recent record close, it rose in just seven.

I measured each year from the close on the Friday before Thanksgiving to the close on the Friday after, with the index's daily closes from Yahoo Finance (which count price moves only, not dividends). That normally includes four trading sessions around the holiday.

Notably, the gains haven't been spread evenly among them. Monday and Tuesday combined averaged a gain of only about 0.1% and finished up in 47 of the 76 years. The Wednesday before Thanksgiving, though, climbed in 58 years and averaged a gain of around 0.3%. The Friday after climbed in 54, with an average gain close to that.

So, the two sessions on either side of the holiday make up almost all the week's typical gain.

The range has been wide, too. The best Thanksgiving week came in 2008, in the middle of the financial crisis, when the index jumped about 12% from around half its record. The worst was 2011, with a loss of nearly 5%.

To match this year's setup, I looked for weeks that began with the index at least 3% but no more than 10% below its record close, with that record set within around four months. Thirteen years qualified, from 1951 to 2025.

The week rose in seven of them, and the average gain was around 0.4%. That's still a little better than a regular week. But it's around half the average for all 76 Thanksgiving weeks, and the hit rate drops from about 70% to about 54%.

The two latest cases went in opposite directions. In 2018, the index began the week around 7% below its September record and dropped nearly 4% more -- and by Dec. 31, it was about 8% below where it started the week.

Last year, it came in about 4% under its Oct. 28 record and climbed nearly 4%.

Also, the rest of the year was better for the group. In 10 of these 13 years, the index closed the year above its level on the Friday before Thanksgiving. Out of all 76 years, the same was true for 54.

For anyone owning an S&P 500 index fund like the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO -3.42%), the next 12 months may matter more than the next four sessions. Twelve of the 13 cases have a full year of data after them, and a year after the Friday before Thanksgiving, the index was up in six and down in six.

These years ranged from a rise of around 24% after 1963 to a drop of about 40% after 2007.

In 2007, the holiday week cost the index only about 1%. But the October record turned out to be the peak before a bear market that cut the index by over half. And 2018's bad week came before a rise of 14% over the next year.

Sure, 13 years is a small sample, and the fall from a 70% hit rate to 54% could easily be chance.

The Vanguard fund finished Nov. 16 around $680, about 5% under the $718.47 peak it hit within the last year.

I think the holiday week's history is a slight edge in investors' favor, and one that has mostly faded in years when the index started below a record. For a long-term owner of the fund, that isn't much to act on either way.

#3010. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2982. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2980. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2967. Interview with the Enemy: Chicago Bears

This week, the New Orleans Saints will head to Solider Field to face the Chicago Bears in Week 11 of the 2026 regular season. To break it down, Bill Zimmerman of Windy City Gridiron joins us to preview this weekend's matchup.

NJ: How legitimate are the Bears' Super Bowl aspirations at this point in the season? Does it feel like this could finally be the year Chicago gets it done?

BZ: The Jeffery Simmons trade changed everything as far as I'm concerned. The Bears defense was the reason they weren't a true Super Bowl contender. Dennis Allen was largely doing it with smoke and mirrors. Yes, he had really effective blitz packages on 3rd down, but that wasn't sustainable in the playoffs against great teams.

The offense was never a question; the defense was. With Simmons, he's a multiplier. In just two games, we've seen the play improve of every defensive lineman and linebacker around him. If their front 7 can play like this, it will help a banged-up secondary that is the weakest part of the team.

No one can guarantee a Super Bowl, but I do think the Bears are on the short list of true Super Bowl contenders this year (and that's very foreign to write).

NJ: Who are some notable names who will be sidelined for the Bears on Sunday against the Saints? Is there a particular area in which New Orleans could exploit?

BZ: The Bears are the healthiest they've been in a while heading into this game. They have RB Kyle Monangai returning, which will be a huge boost for their running game, and rookie CB Malik Muhammad may not be the biggest name, but he is a key starter in their secondary.

TE Cole Kmet is out for the year, and S Coby Bryant has been out since training camp. The only other notable starter who will be out is CB Kyler Gordon, who injured his shoulder against Tampa; he did finish the game, but he is now listed as out for this one.

The key to stopping the Bears is to attack their cornerbacks. It's the weakest part of the roster, but you have to make sure you are getting the ball out quickly before Jeffery Simmons collapses the pocket.

NJ: How much of an impact has Dennis Allen had on turning around the Bears' defense throughout his tenure? Have there been any decisions or coaching tendencies that have rubbed fans the wrong way?

BZ: I think Dennis Allen is very popular among Bears fans, and I think he was the perfect DC hire with Ben Johnson. I think Dennis Allen is at a point where he almost certainly isn't going to get another head coaching job. That means he should be able to pair with Johnson and be the top coaches on this coaching staff for years to come. If things go well, they'll have an Andy Reid/Steve Spagnuolo type partnership.

I think, before the Simmons trade, people had been frustrated with how Dennis Allen wanted to build his defensive line, but other than that, I think Bears fans are very happy with how he calls a game, and I think most of us understand that he got a lot more success out of his defense than the level of talent that was on the field.

The best thing he's done in Chicago is create a phenomenal third down defense. The Bears have been quite strong on third downs and it's forcing teams to get big chunk plays on the early downs or end up punting the ball back to the Bears.

NJ: Who is one Bears player you think Saints fans should keep an eye on Sunday—someone who may not receive much national attention but could have a strong performance?

BZ: Offensively, I don't think there are many names to give because Caleb Williams, Luther Burden, Colston Loveland, and Rome Odunze are their main weapons, and I think football fans are pretty familiar with all of them. I do think Kyle Monangai is going to be a big part of the offensive game plan and split carries with D'Andre Swift so Monangai is one returning from injury that I think can have a strong game.

Defensively, I mentioned Muhammad, but I'll give you Austin Booker. He was a 5th-round pick in 2024 out of Missouri, and he's been coming into his own this year as an improved run defender and a solid pass rusher. You should hear Booker's name at least once on Sunday, either making a TFL or a sack.

NJ: According to FanDuel Sportsbook, the Bears (-720) are currently 11.5-point favorites over the Saints (+520) in Sunday's matchup; what is your final score prediction and how do you see the game playing out?

BZ: It's really hard to imagine that the Bears don't win this one. Ben Johnson is great on extra rest, and the Bears are healthy, and I don't think it's an insult to say the Bears are simply a better team than the Saints and are playing them at home. I think the question isn't who will win or whether the Bears cover the big spread?

I think the offense is going to have success regardless. I think it comes down to if you think the Saints can get points on the Bears' defense. If you do, then there's no reason to think this one couldn't be like 31 to 21 or so, and the Saints cover. Personally, I do think the Saints will struggle on offense against the Bears, and I think the Bears will win this one comfortably by 2 or 3 scores. I'll call it 31 to 13.

Thank you again to Bill Zimmerman for joining us to preview this week's matchup! You can check out the rest of his work here.

#2962. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2956. 5 Things I Think I Think About the Miami Dolphins After Week 10

If you're looking for a silver lining after the Miami Dolphins' 24-22 loss to the Indianapolis Colts, I have good news: The franchise may have found themselves a new hero, and it's Zane Gonzalez. Here are 5 things I think I think after the head-shaking loss to the Daniel Jones-led Colts.

At Nashville's Nissan Stadium, the New York Jets just won their first Super Bowl in more than 50 years. Gang Green is celebrating: Champagne, laughter, tears. Three years earlier, the Colts had traded away the pick that became the No. 1 selection in the 2027 draft. Now the quarterback chosen with that pick has delivered New York a championship. In the on-air interview, head coach Aaron Glenn hugs his third-year superstar quarterback, yelling joyously to the cameras, "We're only here because of this guy! He put us on his back and carried us to become world champions!"

The words "world champions" echo in the brain of thirty-four-year-old Miami Subs employee Zane Gonzalez as he shuts off the black-and-white television in his parents' basement.

Zane takes in all the Miami Dolphins gear around his room – his prized foam finger from 1984, the 8-inch T.D. stuffy on his pillow – and sighs. "Where did we go wrong, T.D.?" he asks his stuffy. "How did the Jets win the Super Bowl while we're still looking for a playoff win?"

"You're right, it was that 2027 draft. That win over the Colts knocked us out of the running for the top pick in the draft! We should have been the Super Bowl winners this year!" He looks up. "T.D., I know what I must do."

Six months later, after convincing Dolphins owner Stephen Ross to invest $500 million in temporal engineering, Gonzalez steps into a prototype time machine and travels three years into the past.

His mission is simple: Convince the Dolphins to bring him on as placekicker. And then?

Most importantly: Miss against the Colts.

Make sure Miami loses enough games to stay ahead of the Colts in the draft order. Land the future franchise quarterback who would finally deliver the Dolphins' long-awaited Super Bowl title.

Sacrifice a few meaningless wins today and the Dolphins become champions tomorrow.

After all, no one would willingly miss two field goals and an extra point in a game his team lost by two … right?

Zane Gonzalez stands at South Pointe Park Pier, staring out at the Atlantic.

The day before, the Dolphins lost to the Patriots, finishing off a 2-15 season and securing the first overall pick in the upcoming draft, one win ahead of the sad sack Colts.

He turns. A little girl, maybe six years old, stands behind him in an oversized Ricky Williams jersey. She clutches her well-loved T.D. stuffed dolphin under one arm.

The girl looks him up and down. Then spits at his feet.

"Nice kicking, dill weed." Before he can respond, she pivots and marches away.

Gonzalez watches her disappear down the pier.

Two weeks ago, the Dolphins held a Drake Maye-led Patriots offense to 3 points in the first half. Last week, that same defense limited Detroit's high-powered offense to one of its lowest point totals of the season. And while the final score on Sunday will show the Colts scored 20 points, the reality is a little different. After halftime, Indianapolis managed exactly three offensive points. Daniel Jones finished with fewer than 110 passing yards. The Dolphins intercepted him twice, returned one for a touchdown, forced a fumble and repeatedly forced Indianapolis off the field with three-and-outs. By the fourth quarter, the defense was dragging the rest of the team back into the game whether it wanted to come along or not.

The usual suspects were everywhere. Chris Johnson (59-yard pick six) continues to look like one of the steals of the draft. Michael Taaffe (6 tackles, 1 pass defended) once again found ways to impact the game. Chop Robinson (1 sack, 2 QB hits) consistently disrupted the pocket. Jordyn Brooks (9 tackles, 1 pass defended) was all over the field.

The Dolphins spotted Indianapolis seven points, generated almost nothing in the kicking game, and still found themselves with a chance to win in the final minutes because the defense spent the second half strangling the Colts offense. For a unit that has carried this team for much of the season, it was another outstanding performance.

Remember this scene from the greatest movie of all time?

The Dolphins' defense is now the Bluesmobile. The Dolphins entered the game without Zach Sieler. Then Jordan Phillips got hurt, and is out for the year. Then Zeek Biggers got hurt, and he's out for the year. By the end of the afternoon, Miami was essentially down to Kenneth Grant and Keith Cooper as healthy defensive tackles.

In-game, that degree of attrition eventually catches up with you. Without those defensive tackles, the run defense clearly felt the strain. Indianapolis finished with 172 rushing yards as Jonathan Taylor and Seth McGowan consistently found running lanes. The Dolphins simply didn't have their usual flexibility up front – too many snaps played by too few healthy bodies.

Miami's defense was good enough to win Sunday, but it wasn't healthy enough to win Sunday.

Let's get the obvious part out of the way first: Kyle McCord made mistakes. The strip-sack touchdown changed the game. The late sack in the fourth quarter was costly. Neither play can simply be ignored because he's a rookie.

But: The strip-sack wasn't the result of panic – it was a quarterback trying to make the correct read and reacting a second too slowly. The late sack wasn't a quarterback seeing ghosts as much as a young passer processing pressure incorrectly while trying to work deeper into the progression.

And then there's everything else. McCord converted a third-and-13. He made several NFL-caliber throws. He stayed aggressive. And he didn't fall apart after the strip-sack touchdown put seven points on the board for Indianapolis.

Young quarterbacks have gotten rattled by far less. Instead, McCord kept battling and gave Miami a chance to win. If you're looking for reasons to be optimistic, Sunday's mistakes looked fixable.

Back in August, I was so young and naïve, blinded by optimism that 2026 would lead to a glorious 6-11 season. Sure, we're rebuilding, but at least as the youngest team in the league, we would avoid some of those devastating injuries the league's more geriatric squads had to deal with.

Now? Now we have Kyle McCord handing the ball off to Ollie Gordon behind a leakier-than-expected offensive line. Our defensive tackles are so decimated, that we're down to three starters – Kenneth Grant, Keith Cooper and you, dear reader. Go suit up.

The team is still in evaluation mode, and there's still a lot of meaningful development that can and should happen for our first- and second-year starters. But the pipe dream of 6 wins feels especially unattainable now. At 38%, the Dolphins have the best odds to pick first in the 2027 draft, according to the prediction market platform Kalshi. ESPN gives the team a 41% chance, also first.

I'd like to see the Dolphins win against the Jets the Sunday after Thanksgiving. A two-win season isn't great, but if both wins come against the Jets, that can get me through the offseason.

#2953. Moritz Wagner’s first-ever chance to face his brother Franz is nothing short of ‘awkward’

Brooklyn's Moritz Wagner has been in the NBA for nine years and alive for 20 more, and he had never, ever played against his brother, Franz, in a game that counted.

That is, until Friday night. The Wagner siblings faced off when the Nets hosted Franz's Orlando Magic, and they squared off against each other for the first time.

"It's awkward, I'm not gonna lie. I don't really know how to compartmentalize it, really deal with it, and try to focus on the game plan and see what happens emotionally," Moritz said beforehand. "It's a dilemma because you want him to do well. At the same time, you game plan for him, you prepare to limit him. So, it's an awkward feeling. I've never been in this position.

Brooklyn Nets center Moritz Wagner (13) warms up before an NBA basketball game against the Orlando Magic at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, NY, Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

"It's weird. It's definitely a new role I've got to grow into. But it's also fun, and we talked about that [Thursday]. It's an absolute privilege, and for our family it's really cool because we've established each other as independent NBA players on two different sides and get to play against each other in this league and have 20 people here watching us. So it's very special, and it's gonna be an emotional night for our family."

Moritz played for Michigan and was a 2018 first-round pick by Orlando, where he'd spent his entire career until signing with the Nets this summer.

Franz, four years his junior, had followed in his footsteps and starred for the Wolverines before being picked eighth overall by the Magic in 2021.

Orlando Magic's Franz Wagner handles the ball during an NBA Cup game against the Brooklyn Nets at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y., Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"My whole NBA career I played with him. The first time in my life playing against him," Franz said. "But as weird as it's going to be, it's a blessing to share the court with your family.Not a lot of people get to do that, so that's how I look at it."

The younger Wagner is breaking out this season with Paolo Banchero injured, entering the meeting averaging 24.4 points, 8.5 assists and 6.9 rebounds.

And it was 30.7 points, 8.5 assists and 7.7 boards over his prior six games.

"We've got a new staff, a new system and a lot of clarity on roles and what's expected of everyone. That helps everyone with their rhythm," Franz said.

"He's playing incredible," Moritz said. "New coach, new system, found a way to be a playmaker and scorer at the same time, dominating the pace, the paint. Obviously with an enormous usage rate as well, which you've got to give him credit for the physicality he plays with. One thing that's really underrated about Franz is his defensive ability. There aren't many players that dominate on both ends of the floor like he does. … It's a joy to watch him as a big brother, regardless of my situation."

Young fan Dylan wears a custom jersey featuring Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner and Brooklyn Nets center Moritz Wagner in Brooklyn, N.Y., Nov. 20, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

That situation is serving as backup center and veteran voice on a struggling Nets squad.

Many have suggested that Brooklyn could target Franz if Orlando breaks him and Banchero up. But for the moment, the Nets have to figure out their own woes internally, and Franz was the enemy Friday.

"I grew up with him. When he's mad, when I see that look in his eyes, it takes me back to when we were 9 and 5 and playing against each other. I don't think I know a human better than him. So on an emotional level, just knowing what he goes through with every step, it's weird to be the opponent," Moritz said.

"Obviously, [he's] a big part of my development as a person and as a player," Franz said. "So it should be fun out there."

Orlando Magic forward Franz Wagner (22) warms up before a game against the Brooklyn Nets on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, N.Y. Robert Sabo for NY Post

The Wagner family was on hand to watch Friday. They even had specialized jerseys made with both of the brothers' names and team colors, and their mother Beate is especially nervous.

"We made these corny split jerseys," Moritz said. "I know my mom's going to go through it [Friday]. I know that. … That's going to be really difficult for her. Definitely a new spot for her. … But she's going to figure that out, and it's also a really good problem to have, obviously.."

#2947. Celtics could be without two starters against Kings

SACRAMENTO — The Celtics could be without Jayson Tatum and Derrick White when they face the Sacramento Kings on Saturday night.

Tatum is listed as questionable due to rest, while White is listed as questionable with a left AC joint contusion (the acromioclavicular joint connects the collarbone to the top of the shoulder blade).

The Celtics are playing on the first night of a back-to-back; they face the Kings on Saturday at 10pm ET, and then the Golden State Warriors on Sunday at 8:30pm ET.

White has yet to miss any games this season; he's averaging 12.7 points, 5 assists, and 3 rebounds per game, while shooting 42.5% from the field and 30.6% from three.

Tatum has missed one game to rest; he's averaging 26.4 points, 6.8 assists, and 6.6 rebounds, shooting 42.8% from the field and 33.3% from three.

Baylor Scheierman has started two games this season and would be a likely candidate to start in the event that either Tatum or White is sidelined. Scheierman is averaging 5.7 points, 3.7 rebounds, and 1.5 assists in 19.4 minutes per game.

Payton Pritchard, who has yet to start a game this season, could also potentially start. Pritchard is averaging 13.7 points, 3.8 assists, and 3.1 rebounds in 27.3 minutes per game, the fourth-most on the team.

It's also possible that Jordan Walsh will see a reduced role on Saturday; Walsh, who has started 10 of 15 games this year, played less than 5 minutes in Wednesday's loss to the Nuggets.

In that event, Sam Hauser or Hugo Gonzalez could see increased opportunities; Hauser is averaging 8.4 points and 3.1 rebounds in 20.3 minutes per game, while Gonzalez is averaging 3.9 points and 2.5 rebounds in 12.9 minutes per game. Hauser has been lights out from three so far this season, shooting 46.8% from beyond the arc.

When they face the Celtics, the Kings will be playing on the second night of a back-to-back; they face the Los Angeles Lakers on Friday night. The Kings have the second-worst record in the NBA at 1-13.

Celtics-Kings tips off at 10pm ET on Saturday night.

#2945. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551

#2944. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2943. Eye of the Tiger: Pivotal position matchups for LSU vs. Tennessee

Although the College Football Playoff wishes have sailed for LSU, there's still very much at stake in this game. Sitting at #9 with an 8-2 record, Tennessee is in a great spot to make the 12-team playoff for the second time under Josh Huepel. Each of their losses were by just a field goal. The first came at the end of September to then #1 Texas, then on the road at South Carolina. They have won 5 of their last 6, which is impressive considering they made a quarterback change 5 weeks ago.

The Volunteers entered 2026 with a massive question mark at the QB position. They felt strongly about last year's starter, Joey Aguilar, being able to get another year of eligibility, but he didn't. They then turned their attention to none other than…Sam Leavitt. We know how that story went, though. Lane Kiffin flew to Knoxville to meet Leavitt in the airport and convince him to commit to LSU and the rest is history.

True freshman Faizon Brandon started the first 5 games of the season for Tennessee, but it was never pretty. So, Huepel turned to redshirt freshman George MacIntyre and he's gone 4-1 as the starter.

LSU heads to Tennessee with a disappointing 6-4 record after a crushing collapse at home against Texas last week. Their season is effectively over and they are playing for pride for these final two regular season games.

We'll get our first peek at what Lane Kiffin's coaching looks like when things are going poorly. Will the Tigers come out fired up and looking to play spoiler? Or will they cower and lose their third straight?

We'll find out starting at 2:30 pm on Saturday in Neyland Stadium and here are three key matchups to look out for:

This one is pretty broad, but things are at an all time low for Leavitt. He was criticized heavily in the media for leaving the field prior to the alma mater as the team's leader, there were anti-Leavitt chants by students as they exited the stadium, and when given the ability to clear the air about some of when speaking publicly on Monday, he avoided it.

Now, he heads to Knoxville, the place he left high and dry to come back down to Baton Rouge. The Tennessee defense is ranked 3rd in total defense, 1st in scoring defense, and 1st against the pass. They sack the quarterback as much as anyone in the conference other than LSU, sitting just one short of the Tigers. It's a tough matchup, in a tough environment, in a tough time for Leavitt.

LSU offensive line vs. Tennessee defensive line

As I just mentioned, the Volunteers can get after the quarterback. They've racked up 39 sacks in 10 games. While 7 of those did come from Tyree Weathersby Sr, who is out for the season, they have plenty of other dudes that can get it done.

Xavier Gilliam and Mariyon Dye have 6 and 5 sacks, respectively, on the defensive line. Although he's listed as a linebacker, Arion Carter can come off of the edge and has 4 sacks of his own.Sam Leavitt was clearly hobbled last week against Texas. It looked to be his right leg/ankle, which is different from the left hamstring that he was successfully playing through earlier in the season. He looked hesitant to run and became scared to throw the ball after his third interception, putting his offensive line in a tough spot where they had to protect while he scrambled around in the backfield. That led to a few holding penalties when he exited the pocket before they expected him to.

Where this somehow gets even more concerning is when LSU tries to run the ball. As good as Dilin Jones is, he's had to make a lot of his magic on his own by making defenders miss. With Leavitt potentially compromised and the Tigers being in a hostile environment, they badly need to find a rushing attack to keep the Jim Knowles defense honest.

Blake Baker's run defense has been excellent all season. Despite missing Whit Weeks since week 4, they've been able to limit opposing running backs on a consistent basis. They've mostly been able to limit opposing offenses, in general, all season, but even when the other team has been able to move the ball, it's typically not on the ground. They're ranked #2 in the SEC in rush defense, allowing just 86.1 yards per game. Opponents are averaging just 2.73 yards per carry.

This week presents a true challenge, though. Allow me to introduce you to DeSean Bishop, the SEC's leading rusher. Wearing #18 for the Volunteers, Bishop has rushed for 1,163 yards with 13 touchdowns. He's averaging 5.1 yards per carry and has very seldom been slowed down. The only team that has limited him was Texas, who held him to 34 yards rushing in week 4.

Bishop has surpassed the century mark in rushing yards in 6 out of 10 games this season. In 2 of the games that he didn't, he finished with 97 yards. The only outliers are that game against Texas and their week 3 game against Kennesaw State, in which he had 9 carries for 42 yards and a touchdown before they took him out of the blowout and let then backup Daune Morris run for 116 yards.

The bottom line is, Tennessee can run the ball. Their offensive line is playing like one of the best in the country. They've only thrown the ball for over 200 yards twice the entire season. Brandon did it in the season opener against Furman and MacIntyre did it in his first start at Arkansas. Aside from that, the Volunteers have gotten to 8-2 and #9 in the country by running Bishop and playing defense.

#2941. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2937. Hawks keep NBA Cup chances alive, defeat Hornets 111-97

The Atlanta Hawks were in action on Friday evening to face the Charlotte Hornets in an NBA Cup game. The Hawks came into this game 1-1 in group play, while the Hornets are 0-2 and looking for their first win. This was a big game for both teams if they wanted to stay in the race.

The Hawks were still without Kingston Flemings for the third straight game, while the Hornets were without Kon Knueppel, Grayson Allen, Dennis Schroder and Grant Williams.

Jalen Johnson got it started for the Hawks, turning defense into offense for an easy two points.

Dyson Daniels was up next for the Hawks, and he knocked down a 3-pointer to keep things rolling.

It was a six-point lead for the Hawks, but the Hornets were able to erase that quickly, and also extended their own lead to as much as eight halfway through the quarter.

The Hornets continued to extend their lead as the Hawks tried to find some rhythm on offense. They were able to do that late in the quarter and cut down their deficit. Going into the second, the Hawks trailed 31-23.

The Hawks went on a run to start the quarter, and it began with their defense. It was the unit that led the charge, and Zuby Ejiofor threw down a huge dunk over two defenders.

Daniels then got a steal, and it turned into an Aaron Wiggins 3-pointer.

The Hawks had a stretch where they were playing well, and then the Hornets put their foot on the gas and extended their lead once again. The Hawks did their best to stay in it, and Johnson turned up his level of play.

Johnson knocked down a 3-pointer during the Hawks' run.

The Hawks found themselves back in it, and they went into halftime trailing by just 57-56.

It was back and forth for both teams midway through the quarter, with neither team truly taking advantage. The Hawks started to pull away just a little, and it helped with Daniels creating this turnover for Johnson to get the dunk plus the foul.

As the quarter went on, the Hawks began to create more separation, and it was the second unit that led the way.

Going into the fourth, the Hawks led 87-76.

The Hawks continued to extend their lead in the fourth, and they got it to as much as 19 points. Daniels found Aaron Wiggins on the wing for this 3-pointer.

Johnson kept finding an open lane to the rim, and he did it off his fourth steal of the game.

The Hornets made their run down the stretch, but the Hawks continued to execute down the stretch. It helped them to ultimately win the game, and they had a solid point differential in the end to help them in the NBA Cup standings.

Johnson finished with 26 points, six rebounds and six assists, Wiggins finished with 19 points, Okongwi finished with 16 points and seven rebounds, and Daniels finished with 11 points, eight rebounds and 10 assists.

The Hawks will be back in action on Sunday afternoon against the Detroit Pistons.

#2928. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551
